## GHSA-26vp-8gxg-v4pg · org.xwiki.rendering:xwiki-rendering-xml

2026-09-18 · Java · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of user-controlled raw content. The `printRaw` method directly processed input strings, allowing an attacker to inject specific sequences like `{{/html}}` which could be combined with other content to prematurely close an HTML block and execute arbitrary code or script within the XWiki rendering engine.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution or script injection within the XWiki rendering context, potentially leading to full system compromise or defacement of rendered content.

### Key diff

```
-		super.printRaw(raw);
+		String escapedRaw = raw.replace("{{/html}}", "&#123;&#123;/html}}");
+
+		StringBuilder prefix = new StringBuilder();
+		for (Character nextChar : List.of('{', '/', 'h', 't', 'm', 'l', '}', '}')) {
+			prefix.append(nextChar);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit escaping for the `{{/html}}` sequence and its prefixes within the `printRaw` method. It replaces `{{/html}}` with `&#123;&#123;/html}}` and also checks for partial matches at the end of the raw content, escaping the opening brace to prevent the sequence from being formed by concatenation.

## GHSA-c8w2-fgvx-vhv4 · github.com/kcp-dev/kcp

2026-09-18 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The kcp front-proxy failed to strip `X-Remote-*` identity headers from incoming requests when no authenticated user was present in the request context. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary identity headers, which were then trusted by downstream components.

### Impact

An authenticated client could inject `X-Remote-Group` headers to impersonate `system:masters` or other privileged groups in any workspace, leading to full administrative control.

### Key diff

```
Before:
```go
func WithProxyAuthHeaders(delegate http.Handler, userHeader, groupHeader string, extraHeaderPrefix string) http.Handler {
	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if u, ok := request.UserFrom(r.Context()); ok {
			authheaders.SetAuthHeaders(r.Header, u, userHeader, groupHeader, extraHeaderPrefix)
		}
		delegate.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	}
}
```

After:
```go
func WithProxyAuthHeaders(delegate http.Handler, userHeader, groupHeader string, extraHeaderPrefix string) http.Handler {
	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if u, ok := request.UserFrom(r.Context()); ok {
			authheaders.SetAuthHeaders(r.Header, u, userHeader, groupHeader, extraHeaderPrefix)
		} else {
			authheaders.ClearAuthHeaders(r.Header, userHeader, groupHeader, extraHeaderPrefix)
		}
		delegate.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	}
}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `ClearAuthHeaders` function and ensures that `X-Remote-*` identity headers are always stripped from incoming requests, regardless of whether an authenticated user is present. This prevents unauthenticated or unprivileged users from injecting arbitrary identity information.

## GHSA-2vh9-42vm-xmv2 · lmdeploy

2026-09-18 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The application used `zmq.Socket.recv_pyobj()` to receive data over a ZeroMQ connection. This method internally uses Python's `pickle.loads()` function, which is known to be insecure when deserializing data from untrusted sources, as it can execute arbitrary code embedded in the pickled payload.

### Impact

An attacker could send a specially crafted pickled object to the ZeroMQ endpoint, leading to arbitrary code execution on the server running the LMDeploy application. This grants the attacker full control over the compromised system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lmdeploy/pytorch/disagg/conn/engine_conn.py
+++ b/lmdeploy/pytorch/disagg/conn/engine_conn.py
-            req: DistServeCacheFreeRequest = await self.p2p_receiver[remote_engine_id].recv_pyobj()
-            if isinstance(req, DistServeCacheFreeRequest):
+            try:
+                raw = await receiver.recv_json()
+                req = DistServeCacheFreeRequest.model_validate(raw)
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the use of `zmq.Socket.recv_pyobj()` and `zmq.Socket.send_pyobj()` with `zmq.Socket.recv_json()` and `zmq.Socket.send_json()`. It also adds `pydantic.ValidationError` handling and uses `DistServeCacheFreeRequest.model_validate()` to ensure that incoming JSON payloads conform to the expected schema, preventing both deserialization RCE and malformed message processing.

## GHSA-xcw4-53cc-hv32 · mnemosyne-memory

2026-09-18 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The original implementation of JWT validation in Mnemosyne's sync server was incomplete. It only decoded the payload and checked for expiration, but crucially, it did not verify the JWT signature. This allowed an attacker to craft arbitrary JWTs with any payload, including an 'exp' claim in the past, and bypass authentication.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass authentication to the sync server, potentially gaining unauthorized access to sensitive data or functionality managed by the Mnemosyne application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/mnemosyne/core/sync_server.py
+++ b/mnemosyne/core/sync_server.py
@@ -101,29 +155,11 @@ def _check_auth(self) -> bool:
             if not auth.startswith("Bearer "):
                 self._send_error(401, "Missing Bearer token")
                 return False
-            token = auth[7:]
+
             try:
-                # Minimal JWT decode without dependencies
-                import base64 as _b64
-                parts = token.split(".")
-                if len(parts) != 3:
-                    self._send_error(401, "Invalid JWT format")
-                    return False
-                # Decode payload (part 1, 0-indexed)
-                payload_b64 = parts[1]
-                # Fix padding
-                pad = 4 - len(payload_b64) % 4
-                if pad != 4:
-                    payload_b64 += "=" * pad
-                payload_bytes = _b64.urlsafe_b64decode(payload_b64)
-                payload = json.loads(payload_bytes.decode("utf-8"))
-                # Check expiry
-                exp = payload.get("exp", 0)
-                if exp and exp < datetime.now().timestamp():
-                    self._send_error(401, "Token expired")
-                    return False
+                self._validate_jwt(auth[7:])
                 return True
-            except Exception as e:
+            except ValueError as e:
                 self._send_error(401, f"JWT validation failed: {e}")
                 return False
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a comprehensive `_validate_jwt` method that performs full HS256 JWT signature verification using the configured shared secret. It also checks the algorithm, decodes parts safely, and validates the expiration time. The `_check_auth` method now calls this new validation logic.

## GHSA-qg2g-g9w3-m5h8 · github.com/stacklok/toolhive

2026-09-18 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The application allowed containerized MCP servers to connect to 'host.docker.internal' by default, even when network isolation was enabled. This special DNS name resolves to the host machine's IP address from within a Docker container, effectively bypassing intended network restrictions and allowing containers to reach services on the host.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage this to perform lateral movement, accessing services running on the host machine from within a compromised container, potentially leading to further compromise of the system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/renderer/src/features/registry-servers/lib/orchestrate-run-registry-server.tsx
+++ b/renderer/src/features/registry-servers/lib/orchestrate-run-registry-server.tsx
@@ -48,8 +71,12 @@ export function prepareCreateWorkloadData(
     secrets,
     cmd_arguments: data.cmd_arguments || [],
     target_port: server.target_port,
-    network_isolation: networkIsolation,
+    network_isolation: networkAccess === NETWORK_ACCESS_MODES.Proxy,
     permission_profile,
+    allow_docker_gateway:
+      networkAccess === NETWORK_ACCESS_MODES.Proxy
+        ? allowHostAccess
+        : undefined,
     volumes,
     // Tag the request as a registry install so the API records the source
     // registry on the workload. `name` is the workload name (user-editable
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an explicit 'allow_docker_gateway' flag in the workload creation request. By default, connections to Docker gateway addresses (like host.docker.internal) are now blocked when network isolation is enabled, requiring an explicit opt-in to allow such access.

## GHSA-m6c8-jcw2-5r25 · paella-core

2026-09-18 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Paella player did not properly sanitize user-supplied caption cue text from WebVTT/DFXP files before rendering it. This allowed an attacker to inject malicious scripts into the captions, which would then be stored and executed in the context of other users' browsers when they viewed the video.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the victim's browser, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
No direct sanitization logic is visible in the provided diff, which primarily contains minor code style changes, null checks, and logging updates. The core fix for XSS in caption cue text is likely in a different part of the codebase or a dependency update not shown here.
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by ensuring that caption cue text is properly sanitized before being displayed in the player. Although the provided diff does not directly show the sanitization logic, the advisory indicates that the fix involves sanitizing the WebVTT/DFXP caption cue text.

## GHSA-3753-m2x2-q623 · msdoc-viewer

2026-09-18 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The legacy DOC renderer did not properly sanitize user-controlled hyperlink schemes (e.g., javascript:, vbscript:, data:, file:, blob:, protocol-relative URLs) and potentially malicious attributes (e.g., onmouseover) when rendering documents. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript into the DOM.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the context of the user's browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement of the application.

### Key diff

```
-window.__rendererSentinel = { markdown: 0, pptx: 0 }
+window.__rendererSentinel = { markdown: 0, pptx: 0, doc: 0 }
 
+const maliciousFont = 'Safe Font" onmouseover="window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 1'
+const docParagraph = (href, text) => ({
+  type: 'paragraph',
+  text,
+  rawProperties: [],
+  styleId: 0,
+  styleName: 'Normal',
+  paraProps: [],
+  paraState: {},
+  tableProps: [],
+  tableState: {},
+  segments: [],
+  inlines: [{
+    type: 'text',
+    text,
+    href,
+    style: { fontFamily: maliciousFont, underline: 0 },
+  }],
+})
+const docParsed = {
+  blocks: [
+    docParagraph('javascript:window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 10', 'unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('JaVa\nScRiPt:window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 20', 'obfuscated unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('vbscript:window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 30', 'vbscript unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('data:text/html,<script>window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 40</script>', 'data unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('file:///tmp/unsafe', 'file unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('blob:https://example.com/unsafe', 'blob unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('//example.com/protocol-relative', 'protocol relative unsafe'),
+    docParagraph('/relative/doc', 'safe root relative'),
+    docParagraph('./relative/doc', 'safe current relative'),
+    docParagraph('../relative/doc', 'safe parent relative'),
+    docParagraph('relative/doc', 'safe plain relative'),
+    docParagraph('https://example.com/doc', 'safe external'),
+    docParagraph('http://example.com/doc', 'safe http'),
+    docParagraph('mailto:security@example.com', 'safe email'),
+    docParagraph('tel:+12025550123', 'safe phone'),
+    docParagraph('#bookmark', 'safe bookmark'),
+    {
+      ...docParagraph(null, 'safe media'),
+      inlines: [
+        {
+          type: 'image',
+          href: 'javascript:window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 50',
+          asset: { name: 'image', mimeType: 'image/gif', dataUrl: pixel, sourceUrl: pixel },
+          style: {},
+        },
+        {
+          type: 'attachment',
+          href: 'data:text/html,unsafe',
+          asset: {
+            name: 'report" onmouseover="window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 60',
+            mimeType: 'application/octet-stream',
+            dataUrl: 'data:application/octet-stream;base64,AA==',
+          },
+          style: {},
+        },
+        {
+          type: 'attachment',
+          href: null,
+          asset: {
+            name: 'unsafe-download.html',
+            mimeType: 'text/html',
+            dataUrl: 'data:text/html;base64,PHNjcmlwdD5hbGVydCgxKTwvc2NyaXB0Pg==',
+          },
+          style: {},
+        },
+      ],
+    },
+    {
+      type: 'attachments',
+      items: [
+        { name: 'safe.bin', mimeType: 'application/octet-stream', dataUrl: 'data:application/octet-stream;base64,AA==' },
+        { name: 'unsafe.svg', mimeType: 'image/svg+xml', dataUrl: 'data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBvbmxvYWQ9ImFsZXJ0KDEpIi8+' },
+        { name: 'unsafe-link', mimeType: 'application/octet-stream', dataUrl: 'javascript:window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 70' },
+      ],
+    },
+  ],
+  warnings: [],
+  assets: [],
+  meta: {},
+}
+const docBlocked = renderMsDoc(docParsed)
+const docAllowed = renderMsDoc(docParsed, { externalLinkPolicy: 'allow' })
+const docDirectTemplate = document.createElement('template')
+docDirectTemplate.innerHTML = docAllowed.html
+const docTarget = document.querySelector('#doc')
+mountMsDoc(docTarget, {
+  ...docAllowed,
+  html: docAllowed.html + '<img id="doc-event" src="' + pixel + '" onload="window.__rendererSentinel.doc += 100">',
+})
 
 const markdown = [
   '# Safe Markdown heading',
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new sanitization function, `sanitizeMsDocLinkHref`, to filter out unsafe hyperlink schemes and attributes in the DOC renderer. It also adds comprehensive test cases to verify that various malicious link types and attribute injections are blocked, ensuring that only safe URLs and attributes are rendered.

## GHSA-7q85-xj36-vmfc · adm-zip

2026-09-18 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `adm-zip` library did not properly cap the output length during decompression when using the streaming API. While a `maxOutputLength` option was passed to `zlib` for Node.js versions >= 15, the streaming API in `zlib` ignores this option, allowing an attacker to provide a ZIP entry with a small compressed size but a very large declared uncompressed size, leading to excessive memory allocation during decompression.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious ZIP file (a 'decompression bomb') that, when processed by the vulnerable library, would consume an inordinate amount of memory, leading to a denial of service for the application or system hosting the `adm-zip` process.

### Key diff

```
--- a/methods/inflater.js
+++ b/methods/inflater.js
@@ -36,6 +36,11 @@
             tmp.on("data", function (data) {
                 parts.push(data);
                 total += data.length;
+                if (total > maxOutputLength) {
+                    return fail(Errors.MAX_OUTPUT_EXCEEDED());
+                }
+                parts.push(data);
             });
             tmp.on("end", function () {
                 if (done) return;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a manual check for `total > maxOutputLength` within the `data` event handler of the decompression stream. If the accumulated decompressed data exceeds the `maxOutputLength` (derived from the declared uncompressed size, with a minimum of 1 byte), the decompression process is immediately terminated, preventing excessive memory allocation. A new error message `MAX_OUTPUT_EXCEEDED` was also added.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 172 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Python, JavaScript, RUST, Java, C#, PHP, Ruby, Erlang

## GHSA-gjw4-3v3v-rqxg · github.com/projectcapsule/capsule

2026-09-18 · Go · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The Capsule Kubernetes multi-tenancy solution failed to properly enforce configured rules for Service resources, specifically regarding forbidden service types and external names. This omission allowed tenant owners to create services that should have been restricted by the Capsule configuration.

### Impact

An attacker (tenant owner) could bypass Capsule's security policies to create services with forbidden types (e.g., LoadBalancer, NodePort) or external names, potentially exposing internal services or resources, or enabling unauthorized network access.

### Key diff

```
--- a/cmd/controller/main.go
+++ b/cmd/controller/main.go
@@ -583,6 +597,7 @@ func main() {
 		),
 		route.Service(
 			service.Handler(
+				servicerules.ServiceRules(regexCache),
 				service.Validating(),
 			),
 		),
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new validation webhooks for Kubernetes Service resources. It adds `servicerules.ServiceRules` to the service webhook handler and a new `route.RulesValidating` webhook, ensuring that service types and external names are validated against the configured Capsule policies.

## GHSA-f94q-w3w8-cj67 · github.com/projectcapsule/capsule

2026-09-18 · Go · **MODERATE** 6.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The webhook validation for Tenant resources, specifically for `AllowedHostnames` and `ForbiddenAnnotations`/`ForbiddenLabels` regexes, was using the old (stale) version of the Tenant object during an update operation. This meant that if an attacker submitted an update with an invalid or malicious regex, the webhook would validate the *previous* valid regex, allowing the invalid one to be saved. When a subsequent operation tried to use this saved malicious regex, it could lead to a ReDoS.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the webhook validation to store a malicious regular expression in the Tenant configuration. This could then lead to a Regular Expression Denial of Service (ReDoS) when the stored regex is later compiled or used, potentially causing resource exhaustion or crashes in components that process these regexes.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/webhook/tenant/validation/hostname_regex.go
+++ b/internal/webhook/tenant/validation/hostname_regex.go
@@ -54,8 +54,8 @@ func (h *hostnameRegexHandler) OnDelete(
 func (h *hostnameRegexHandler) OnUpdate(
 	_ client.Client,
 	_ client.Reader,
-	old *capsulev1beta2.Tenant,
 	tnt *capsulev1beta2.Tenant,
+	_ *capsulev1beta2.Tenant,
 	decoder admission.Decoder,
 	_ events.EventRecorder,
 ) handlers.Func {
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `OnUpdate` handler for `hostnameRegexHandler` to correctly receive and validate the *new* Tenant object, rather than the old one. Additionally, the `forbiddenAnnotationsRegexHandler` was updated to ensure that empty regex strings are skipped and that the correct regex string is passed to `regexp.Compile` for validation, preventing compilation errors for empty strings and improving error messages.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 16 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript, Python, PHP
> → GHSA-fmwg-qcqh-m992
> → GHSA-cjw9-ghj4-fwxf
> → GHSA-fwjq-xwfj-gv75

## GHSA-j8px-rmrx-76h9 · github.com/caddyserver/caddy/v2

2026-09-18 · Go · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because Caddy's rewrite module would re-expand placeholder syntax within an already-expanded query string. If an attacker could inject placeholder syntax (e.g., '{env.SECRET}') into the query string via a request header or other input, the server would re-evaluate it, potentially exposing sensitive environment variables.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious request that, when processed by the rewrite module, would cause Caddy to re-evaluate injected placeholder syntax. This could lead to the disclosure of sensitive information, such as environment variables (e.g., API keys, database credentials) configured on the server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/modules/caddyhttp/rewrite/rewrite.go
+++ b/modules/caddyhttp/rewrite/rewrite.go
@@ -223,6 +223,15 @@ func (rewr Rewrite) Rewrite(r *http.Request, repl *caddy.Replacer) bool {
 			newPath, injectedQuery = before, after
 			// don't overwrite explicitly-configured query string
 			if query == "" {
+				// the injected query came from the first-pass placeholder
+				// expansion above, which means any '{' or '}' bytes in it
+				// must have come from replacement values (e.g. a request
+				// header), not from operator-written placeholder syntax.
+				// escape them so buildQueryString does not re-expand them,
+				// which would allow attacker input like {env.SECRET} to be
+				// evaluated (see GHSA-j8px-rmrx-76h9).
+				injectedQuery = strings.ReplaceAll(injectedQuery, "{", "%7B")
+				injectedQuery = strings.ReplaceAll(injectedQuery, "}", "%7D")
 				query = injectedQuery
 			}
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by escaping curly braces ('{' and '}') in the injected query string before it is processed by the `buildQueryString` function. This prevents the re-expansion of attacker-controlled placeholder syntax, ensuring that any curly braces in the query string are treated as literal characters rather than as part of a placeholder expression.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 22 occurrences**

## GHSA-w72w-9qmj-c9qm · github.com/anycable/anycable

2026-09-18 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→HARDCODED`

### Root cause

The telemetry subsystem had a hardcoded authentication token, 'secret', which was used by default. Additionally, the telemetry system was designed to transmit CLI arguments, which could include sensitive information like database credentials or other secrets, to a remote endpoint.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially intercept or gain access to the hardcoded token, allowing unauthorized access to the telemetry data. Furthermore, sensitive CLI arguments, if present, would be exfiltrated to a third-party telemetry server, leading to a compromise of confidential information.

### Key diff

```
--- a/telemetry/config.go
+++ b/telemetry/config.go
-var authToken = "secret" // make it overridable during build time
+var auth = "" // provide a secret token during the build time to enable tracking
 
 func NewConfig() *Config {
 	return &Config{
-		Token:       authToken,
+		Token:       auth,
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the hardcoded 'secret' token, replacing it with an empty string, effectively disabling telemetry by default unless a token is explicitly provided during build time. It also adds a check to ensure the telemetry tracker is only enabled and collects data if a token is present, preventing the transmission of data, including CLI arguments, when telemetry is not properly configured.


🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/anycable/anycable/commit/201c67e99e463ed63bd6b345562f4c458385fcee)

## GHSA-gxjc-74v5-3vx3 · github.com/projectcapsule/capsule

2026-09-18 · Go · **MODERATE** 4.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The application failed to validate user-supplied regular expressions for forbidden annotations and labels before compiling them. An attacker could provide a malformed or overly complex regular expression, which, when compiled, could lead to a panic in the admission webhook or consume excessive resources.

### Impact

An attacker with permissions to create or update a Tenant resource could trigger a panic in the Capsule admission webhook, effectively causing a denial of service for namespace admissions and potentially impacting the stability of the Kubernetes cluster.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/webhook/tenant/validation/forbidden_annotations_regex.go
+++ b/internal/webhook/tenant/validation/forbidden_annotations_regex.go
@@ -74,8 +74,16 @@ func (h *forbiddenAnnotationsRegexHandler) validate(tnt *capsulev1beta2.Tenant, req admission.Request) *admission.Response {
 		"annotations": tnt.Spec.NamespaceOptions.ForbiddenAnnotations.Regex,
 	}
 
-	for scope, annotation := range regexesToCheck {
-		if _, err := regexp.Compile(tnt.Spec.NamespaceOptions.ForbiddenLabels.Regex); err != nil {
-			return ad.Denyf("unable to compile %s regex for forbidden %s", annotation, scope)
+	for scope, expression := range regexesToCheck {
+		if expression == "" {
+			continue
+		}
+
+		if _, err := regexp.Compile(expression); err != nil {
+			return ad.Denyf(
+				"unable to compile regex %q for forbidden %s: %v",
+				expression,
+				scope,
+				err,
+			)
 		}
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit validation for the regular expressions provided in `ForbiddenLabels.Regex` and `ForbiddenAnnotations.Regex`. Before attempting to compile the regex, it checks if the expression is empty and then attempts compilation, returning an admission denial if compilation fails.

## GHSA-3w57-8xmc-8v26 · anyio

2026-09-18 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The `open_process` function in AnyIO was incorrectly assigning the `group` parameter value to `extra_groups` instead of the intended `extra_groups` parameter. This meant that any supplementary groups specified by the user in `extra_groups` were ignored, and the child process would inherit the parent process's supplementary groups.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially run a child process with unintended supplementary group privileges inherited from the parent process, leading to privilege escalation if the parent process had elevated group memberships.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/anyio/_core/_subprocesses.py
+++ b/src/anyio/_core/_subprocesses.py
@@ -176,7 +176,7 @@ async def open_process(
         kwargs["group"] = group
 
     if extra_groups is not None:
-        kwargs["extra_groups"] = group
+        kwargs["extra_groups"] = extra_groups
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the assignment within the `open_process` function. It changes `kwargs["extra_groups"] = group` to `kwargs["extra_groups"] = extra_groups`, ensuring that the `extra_groups` parameter is correctly passed to the subprocess creation mechanism.

## GHSA-5gm3-9crp-6g3v · github.com/f1bonacc1/process-compose

2026-09-18 · Go · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's Server-Sent Events (SSE) listener was directly exposed without proper host or origin validation. This allowed a malicious website to trick a user's browser into making requests to the local SSE server via DNS rebinding, bypassing the same-origin policy.

### Impact

An attacker could control local process-compose MCP tools by making the user's browser send commands to the local SSE server, potentially leading to arbitrary command execution or unauthorized access to local resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/mcp/server.go
+++ b/src/mcp/server.go
@@ -205,12 +210,21 @@ func (s *Server) startSSE() error {
 		Str("address", addr).
 		Msg("Starting MCP server with SSE transport")
 
-	// Create SSE server
+	// Create SSE server. It is served through our own http.Server so we can
+	// wrap it with a Host/Origin/auth trust boundary (see sseSecurityMiddleware)
+	// that guards against browser DNS-rebinding attacks. No WithSSECORS is used:
+	// the middleware gate makes the library's default ACAO:* header moot.
 	sseServer := server.NewSSEServer(s.mcpServer)
+	s.sseServer = sseServer
+	s.httpServer = &http.Server{
+		Addr:    addr,
+		Handler: s.sseSecurityMiddleware(sseServer),
+	}
+	s.warnInsecureSSE()
 
 	// SSE transport blocks, so run in goroutine
 	go func() {
-		if err := sseServer.Start(addr); err != nil {
+		if err := s.httpServer.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) {
 			log.Error().Err(err).Msg("MCP SSE server error")
 		}
 	}()
```

### Fix summary

A new `sseSecurityMiddleware` was introduced to validate the `Host` and `Origin` headers of incoming requests against an allowlist of trusted hosts (loopback addresses and explicitly configured hosts). Additionally, an optional API token authentication was added as a defense-in-depth measure.

## GHSA-5p39-cfhj-2xmp · anyio

2026-09-18 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `anyio` process-pool workers redirected `sys.stdin` and `sys.stdout` to `/dev/null` but failed to do the same for `sys.stderr`. If a worker process wrote a large amount of data to `stderr`, the parent process's pipe buffer could fill up, causing the worker to block indefinitely when attempting to write more data.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause a worker process to block indefinitely, leading to resource exhaustion and a denial of service for applications relying on the `anyio` process pool.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/anyio/to_process.py
+++ b/src/anyio/to_process.py
@@ -211,6 +211,7 @@ def process_worker() -> None:
     stdout = sys.stdout
     sys.stdin = open(os.devnull)
     sys.stdout = open(os.devnull, "w")
+    sys.stderr = open(os.devnull, "w")
 
     stdout.buffer.write(b"READY\n")
     while True:
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by redirecting `sys.stderr` to `/dev/null` within the `process_worker` function, similar to how `sys.stdin` and `sys.stdout` are handled. This prevents `stderr` output from blocking the worker process.

## GHSA-82r6-8w77-94w6 · anyio

2026-09-18 · Python · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The `anyio` library used the outdated IDNA 2003 standard for encoding internationalized domain names (IDNs) when resolving hostnames for TLS connections. IDNA 2003 has known vulnerabilities where certain Unicode characters can be mapped to different ASCII representations than in the updated IDNA 2008 standard, leading to potential hostname mismatches.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious domain name that, when processed by `anyio` using IDNA 2003, resolves to the same ASCII representation as a legitimate domain. This could allow the attacker to present a valid TLS certificate for their malicious domain, tricking the client into believing it's communicating with the legitimate server and enabling man-in-the-middle attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/anyio/streams/tls.py
+++ b/src/anyio/streams/tls.py
@@ -141,11 +141,23 @@ async def wrap(
         bio_in = ssl.MemoryBIO()
         bio_out = ssl.MemoryBIO()
 
+        # Resolve international host names using IDNA 2008.
+        # Otherwise wrap_bio() would resolve them with IDNA 2003.
+        if hostname is not None:
+            from .._core._sockets import idna2008_resolve
+
+            server_hostname: bytes | None = idna2008_resolve(hostname)
+        else:
+            server_hostname = None
+
         # External SSLContext implementations may do blocking I/O in wrap_bio(),
         # but the standard library implementation won't
         if type(ssl_context) is ssl.SSLContext:
             ssl_object = ssl_context.wrap_bio(
-                bio_in, bio_out, server_side=server_side, server_hostname=hostname
+                bio_in,
+                bio_out,
+                server_side=server_side,
+                server_hostname=server_hostname,
             )
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `idna2008_resolve` function that explicitly uses the `idna` library with `uts46=True` to ensure IDNA 2008 (UTS #46) compliant hostname resolution. This function is then used consistently across `getaddrinfo` and `wrap` (for TLS stream wrapping) to ensure all hostname resolutions for network connections adhere to the more secure IDNA 2008 standard.

## GHSA-p5vg-v7mj-f6q4 · github.com/frain-dev/convoy

2026-09-18 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `FindSourceByID` function in the `sources` service fetched a source directly by its ID from the repository without enforcing the `projectID` scope at the database query level. This meant that while the function received a `projectID` parameter, it was not used to filter the results, allowing an attacker to request a source by ID from a different project.

### Impact

An attacker with authenticated access to any project could enumerate and retrieve source details, including plaintext message broker credentials, from other tenants/projects by simply providing the correct source ID.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/sources/impl.go
+++ b/internal/sources/impl.go
@@ -443,7 +443,21 @@ func (s *Service) FindSourceByID(ctx context.Context, projectID, id string) (*da
 		return nil, &ServiceError{ErrMsg: "error retrieving source", Err: err}
 	}
 
-	return rowToSource(row)
+	source, err := rowToSource(row)
+	if err != nil {
+		return nil, err
+	}
+
+	// FetchSourceByID keys only on the source id, so enforce the project scope
+	// here (the sibling FindSourceByName/DeleteSourceByID scope in SQL). Without
+	// this, any authorized caller could read another project's source by id,
+	// including its plaintext broker credentials. Treat a cross-project hit as
+	// not found so ids stay non-enumerable.
+	if source.ProjectID != projectID {
+		return nil, datastore.ErrSourceNotFound
+	}
+
+	return source, nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds an explicit check within the `FindSourceByID` function to compare the `ProjectID` of the retrieved source with the `projectID` provided in the request. If they do not match, it returns a `datastore.ErrSourceNotFound` error, effectively enforcing project-level scoping for source retrieval by ID.
